- **histone-binary-ptm-abundance-statistics**: Use when comparing relative abundances of binary (two-mark) histone peptides between conditions (e.g., wt vs Suz12-/- ESCs) across two histone preparations, to identify significantly changed marks with a t-test. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-proteomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-proteomics/tree/HEAD/collections/proteomics/v1/leaves/histone-binary-ptm-abundance-statistics))
- **histone-combinatorial-ptm-abundance-normalization**: Use when MS/MS spectra of hypermodified histone tails have been identified by Mascot (e.g. middle-down or top-down analyses with scrambled combinatorial PTMs) and you need relative abundance per combinatorial mark. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-proteomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-proteomics/tree/HEAD/collections/proteomics/v1/leaves/histone-combinatorial-ptm-abundance-normalization))
- **histone-h1-weekly-digest**: Weekly PubMed digest of new histone H1 / linker histone literature ([hdbp/claude](https://github.com/hdbp/claude/tree/HEAD/claude-desktop/scheduled-tasks/histone-h1-weekly-digest))
- **histone-mark-correlation-analysis**: Use when you have mESC (or analogous) ChIP-seq BAM files for chromatin marks plus ranked RNA-seq expression data and need to test whether mark signal correlates with gene expression groups (high/moderate/silent). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/histone-mark-correlation-analysis))
- **histone-mark-odds-ratio-computation**: Use when you have two sets of genomic intervals (e.g., true vs predicted regulatory indels) plus interval annotations for chromatin interactions or histone marks and need per-feature enrichment odds ratios. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/histone-mark-odds-ratio-computation))
- **histone-mark-quality-metric-stratification**: Use when you need to compute the fraction of narrowPeak peaks falling in ENCODE union DHS regions per ChIP-seq sample and compare overlap-ratio distributions across histone mark/factor categories as a QC stratification. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/histone-mark-quality-metric-stratification))
- **histone-modification-chipseq-data-processing**: Use when processing ChIP-Seq BED files of histone modifications (e.g., H3K4me3, H3K27me3) to call enriched regions with HPeak and to assess whether different peak-calling algorithms produce overlapping peak sets. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/histone-modification-chipseq-data-processing))
- **histone-modification-differential-region-calling**: Use when comparing ChIP-seq profiles of broad diffuse histone marks (e.g. H3K27me3, H3K9me3, H3K36me3, H3K79me2) between two samples/cell lines and peak-style callers would yield false positives/negatives. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/histone-modification-differential-region-calling))
- **histone-modification-em-mixture-fitting**: Use when comparing ChIP-seq profiles of broad histone modifications (e.g. H3K27me3, H3K9me3) between two samples to call modified or differentially modified regions, and peak-style callers would misclassify diffuse signals. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/histone-modification-em-mixture-fitting))
- **histone-modification-peak-calling-comparison**: Use when a qPCR-validated or otherwise gold-standard ChIP-seq dataset of a broad histone mark (e.g. H3K27me3, H3K9me3) is available and you need to quantify which peak caller detects modified versus unmodified regions via sensitivity-specificity/ROC analysis. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/histone-modification-peak-calling-comparison))
- **histone-modification-peak-profile-processing**: Use when benchmarking peak-shape clustering or alignment methods and you need ground-truth-labelled synthetic peak profiles derived from real ChIP-Seq (e.g. ENCODE H3K4me3 K562) seed regions with known cluster-of-origin labels. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/histone-modification-peak-profile-processing))
- **histone-modification-peak-set-processing**: Use when you have two BED-format ChIP-seq peak lists (chromosome, start, end) for different histone marks and need to partition their union into mark-specific and overlapping classes (e.g. active/bivalent/silent promoters). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/histone-modification-peak-set-processing))
- **histone-modification-state-structure-verification**: Use when reproducing or auditing histoneHMM's emission model: you have binned bivariate ChIP-seq read counts from two samples (e.g. BN vs SHR) and need to verify the four-component copula fit yields coherent modified/unmodified/differential state classes. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/histone-modification-state-structure-verification))
- **histone-peptide-ion-intensity-quantification**: Use when you need relative abundances of hypermodified histone-tail peptides (middle-down/top-down data) from Mascot .csv results, especially where isobaric peptides with different PTM localizations co-fragment and must be deconvoluted. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-proteomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-proteomics/tree/HEAD/collections/proteomics/v1/leaves/histone-peptide-ion-intensity-quantification))
- **histone-ptm-co-occurrence-frequency-analysis**: Use when middle-down histone MS/MS data (Mascot .csv + Histone Coder Brno-nomenclature PTM strings) must be tested for co-existence or mutual exclusivity of specific mark pairs, e.g. H3K9me3K27me3 vs H3K18acK23ac. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-proteomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-proteomics/tree/HEAD/collections/proteomics/v1/leaves/histone-ptm-co-occurrence-frequency-analysis))
- **histone-ptm-relative-quantification**: Use when Mascot .csv search output and a Histone Coder result file are available for histone middle-down ETD spectra and relative abundances of single or combinatorial PTM peptide groups (e.g. 0-3 acetyl, 0-9 methyl) must be quantified across replicates. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-proteomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-proteomics/tree/HEAD/collections/proteomics/v1/leaves/histone-ptm-relative-quantification))
- **histone-tail-lc-separation-qc**: Use when replicate RP/WCX-HILIC LC-MS/MS runs of histone tails are available and run-to-run retention-time stability of H3, H4, and H2A N-terminal tail peaks must be checked against fixed minute-thresholds before PTM quantification. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-proteomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-proteomics/tree/HEAD/collections/proteomics/v1/leaves/histone-tail-lc-separation-qc))
- **histopath-c-eval**: Evaluates the robustness of vision-language models (VLMs) and test-time adaptation (TTA) methods when applied to histopathology images under realistic domain shifts. It probes how well models maintain classification accuracy when exposed to synthetic corruptions like staining variations, dust, blurring, and noise that mimic real-world clinical imaging artifacts. Use when the user wants to benchmark on NCT-7K, NCT-100K, LC25000, SkinCancer, RenalCell, MHIST, or asks about evaluating this task. Reports accuracy. - **histopath-domain-gen-eval**: Evaluates a model's ability to generalize to out-of-distribution domains (different hospitals or staining protocols) in histopathology image classification. Use when the user wants to benchmark on CAMELYON17-WILDS, Epithelium-Stroma, or asks about evaluating this task. Reports Accuracy (%). Use when the user wants to benchmark on BRACS, BACH, BreCaHAD, CRC, or asks about evaluating this task. Reports macro-averaged F1 score. - **histopathology-grading-eval**: Evaluates deep learning models on binary classification of histopathology tissue tiles as malignant or benign. It probes the capacity of multi-stream architectures to capture diverse morphological and textural features for medical image grading. Use when the user wants to benchmark on CAMELYON16, Invasive Ductal Carcinoma (IDC), or asks about evaluating this task. Reports accuracy. - **historial**: CUSTODIO del vertical NICHOS: log append-only de eventos por nicho. Registra cada evento relevante del ciclo de vida de un nicho y sirve la cronologia completa por RPC. Persistencia per-proyecto via PosPersistencia. Carga este modulo cuando necesites registrar un evento del ciclo de vida de un nicho, consultar su cronologia, o cuando otro modulo quiera reaccionar al pulso nichos.historial.registrado. - **historial-proceso-contable**: Skill FULL del módulo CUSTODIO CON PERSISTENCIA `historial-proceso-contable` de la vertical contabilidad (Enki). Registro APPEND-ONLY e inmutable de lo PROCESADO y lo FALLADO de la entrada, con su rastro (asunto/origen/motivo/fases/en_cola). Es el historial del PROCESO de entrada, no del asiento (eso es traza-asiento B4). El resultado (PROCESADO|FALLADO) es obligatorio: sin él no se anota. NADA se borra ni se sobrescribe. Publica el hecho contabilidad.proceso_anotado. Persiste por proyecto vía PosPersistencia. ([Noninapizzicas/3enki](https://github.com/Noninapizzicas/3enki/tree/HEAD/modules/cosecha/cantera/enki/historial-proceso-contable))
- **historian**: Chesterton's Fence for code you're about to delete or refactor. Before you rip out a weird retry, a seemingly dead branch, an ugly workaround, or a "redundant" check whose purpose isn't obvious — find out why it exists first. git blame it, find the callers, read the linked issue/PR/commit. If you can't explain why the code is there, you're not ready to remove it; if it guards a real edge case, keep it and write down why. Supports intensity levels: lite, full (default), ultra.
